**YF Sorular ;**

- Verilen teklifler on-prime kurulum iÃ§indi, cloud kurulum talep edilmesi durumunda teklif tutarÄ±nda ve kurulum sÃ¼resinde deÄŸiÅŸiklik olacak mÄ±?

- OluÅŸturduÄŸumuz (google ads, meta, tiktok etc.) reklam kampanyalarÄ±nÄ± kanal/aracÄ± ve persona bazÄ±nda ayrÄ±ca  grup bazÄ±nda (RFM vb.) adjust ya da baÅŸka bir harici araÃ§ kullanmadan sadece sizin aracÄ±nÄ±zda bulunan dashboard Ã¼zerinden takip edebilecek miyiz? (bkz. Senaryo 1 - 2)

- 2. Sorunun cevabÄ± olumsuz ise reklam kampanyalarÄ±ndan gelen kullanÄ±cÄ±larÄ±n davranÄ±ÅŸlarÄ±/hareketlerini adjust ya da baÅŸka bir harici program Ã¼zerinden mi Ã¶lÃ§eceÄŸiz yoksa sizin analiz aracÄ±nÄ±za entegre olduÄŸu iÃ§in tek dashboardâ€™da gÃ¶rebilecek miyiz? (bkz. Senaryo 2-3)

- Reklam kampanyalarÄ±ndan web ve/veya app mecralarÄ±nÄ±za gelen kullanÄ±cÄ±larÄ±n davranÄ±ÅŸlarÄ±/hareketlerini bizim platformumuz ile Ã¶lÃ§ebilirsiniz. ## BetCity (iGaming)

# BetCity

*Source: BetCity.xlsx*


## Questions

*34 rows Ã— 3 columns*

| Unnamed: 0 | Unnamed: 1 | Unnamed: 2 |
| --- | --- | --- |
|  | BETCITY QUESTIONS | ANSWERS |
|  | 1. Technological stack | 1 |
|  |  |  |
|  | 2. Architectural logical diagram of the relationship between components | 1 |
|  |  |  |
|  | 3. Architectural diagram of physical location of components on servers and links between them, taking into account fault tolerance | 2 |
|  |  |  |
|  | 4. Diagram of integration with the customerâ€™s systems (data sources and consumers, services), data flows, data placement, calculation of aggregates | FTPS, REST API, We can also pull data from data sources such as Oracle, SQL, Postgre, MySQL, Bigquery, Redshift, etc |
|  |  |  |
|  | 5. Calculation of aggregates: in CRM or on the side of the data source? Do you need to transfer a full flow of events or partially pre-aggregated data to CRM? | We can generate aggregates with our Data Automation Center. The platform is able to create any data and create other tables based on the data. So you dont need to create calculated aggregates.  |
|  |  |  |
|  | 6. What flow of incoming events (number of events per second) is the system designed for? What capacities are required to process them, given that historical data may be required to process the event? How is event handling logic implemented? | It completely depends on # of public servers. Completely horizantal growth architecture. We can collect millions of event per sec. By default, the system can collect up to 500K events per min. |
|  |  |  |
|  | 7. The ability to create difficult conditions/aggregates, for example: the amount of the first football bet in the last 30 days, which exceeds 30% of the average bet of this player over the past six months. Or, for example, the first deposit after the player has not placed a bet for 60 days, except for freebets. | With relational database which can keep any data + ability to connect any data source and combining all data together like it is in one place the platform can genarate any complex segment you can name. Besides its easy to use interaction segment interface, it can provides you SQL query tool to select any segment you want. You need to show us where data is or put that data on our platform. That is all we need. |
|  |  |  |
|  | 8. What are the scaling mechanisms? How will the system behave if the flow of incoming events temporarily increases 100 times? | We can put our public servers under kubernates (open shift) and automatically scales when needed.  |
|  |  |  |
|  | 9. What are the mechanisms for ensuring fault tolerance and disaster recovery? What will happen to the integrations? | The system itself is fault tolerance in local. Please see our db cluster structure. We can even work with N DB paralel to maximize fault-tolerance. Also all ciritical services are always behind NLBs.  |
|  |  |  |
|  | 10. How are alerts managed? Direct integration with alert services or through the customerâ€™s intermediate service? | Yes we can integrate with your alert service as long as Nlog supports. We use zabbix and graylog for alert mechanisms. Also use site 7/24 alike service to monitor. |
|  |  |  |
|  | 11. Content management capabilities related to marketing activities on the fronts (mob applications, sites): is there any standard mechanism (for example, a well-developed API that should be supported by the fronts/back of the customerâ€™s system, a UI in CRM to manage content related to promotions, etc.) or do you need to be implemented â€œfrom scratchâ€? Can CRM be an orchestrator for CMS? | No. |
|  |  |  |
|  | 12. Ability to respond in real time to the playerâ€™s actions. | Realtime in app functions based on client parameters and server parameters segment/tags/contact information/aggregated fields. |
|  |  |  |
|  | 13. The ability to create â€œon the flyâ€ individual parameters of shares. For example, the player makes an express, at this time tell him which event to add. Or, for example, at the moment when the player makes a deposit, offer a freebet with an individually calculated amount if the player deposits another x money, etc. |  |
|  |  |  |
|  | 14. As expected, processing (accrual, application) of freebets: in CRM or in the customerâ€™s system. What is the supposed interaction of the systems? |  |
|  |  |  |
|  | 15. What possibilities do you have with promo codes, how you can process and/ or interact with other systems on promo codes? | We create custom nodes in our journeys to integrate your promo codes system. So marketing people only drag and drop it to the joruney to integrate with your promo codes.  |
|  |  |  |
|  | 16. What are the opportunities for loyalty points?  | We can create calculated fields based on the past history and update this point per customer. |
|  |  |  |
|  | 17. Do we need to store the personal data in a CRM? | Personal data can be stored in Dengage master_contact table without any dependency on a CRM source. Personal data can be imported to the platform through the UI through a file or with API.  |

|  | How many dedicated collection libraries does your platform support? |  |
|  | Describe how your platform collects user behavior data across applications, websites, backend systems, and cloud services (online and offline sources). |  |
|  | Describe how your platform prevents data loss and ensures data collection reliability. Describe how your platform does this during times of peak activity. |  |
|  | How many customers deploy collection through multiple channels (ie: web, mobile, server-side)? |  |
|  | How is the data flow visualized in your platform? |  |
|  | Does your platform allow for "no-code" implementation of tracking? |  |
|  | Can you implement data collection without disruption?  |  |
|  | Does your platform support data collection from web through a dedicated javascript SDK? |  |
|  | How many of your customers are using your javascript SDK? |  |
|  | How many of your customers are using your iOS SDK? |  |
|  | Does your platform support data collection from Android mobile platforms through a dedicated Android SDK? |  |
|  | How many of your customers are using your Android SDK? |  |
|  | Does your SDK support basic device data such as:<br>- Device type<br>- Device OS |  |
|  | Does your SDK support custom event logging and collection? |  |
|  | Does your SDK support the automatic collection of App lifecycle events, such as:<br>- Installed<br>- Opened<br>- Updated |  |
|  | How is the SDK collecting, buffering, and distributing events? |  |
|  | What is the maximum message (single event) size? |  |
|  | What is the maximum batch size (aggregated # messages)? |  |
|  | Does your platform support data collection through a generic REST interface? |  |
|  | Does your platform support data collection through webhook subscriptions? |  |
|  | In how many languages do you offer server-side data collection libraries? Please list the languages you support |  |
|  | How does your platform ingest bulk data? |  |
|  | Is there a cost difference between streaming vs batch ingestion? |  |
|  | How does your platform support enriching data in transit with third-party or backend data? |  |
|  | How can your platform ingest object or event data from sources not covered by the platform? |  |
|  | Does your system have the ability to ETL data from leading SaaS providers? (Examples: Salesforce, Zendesk, etc. - Please provide names of supported solutions) |  |
|  | How many customers currently utilize this functionality? |  |
|  | Does your platform impose any rate limiting? |  |
| Data Activation & Output: <br>Describe your platform's ability to deliver data to all of the tools and systems your teams use. |  |  |
|  | How many third-party tools (destinations) do you support? |  |
|  | Can connection points be provisioned programmatically? |  |
|  | Does your platform support real-time (low latency) data forwarding that is publicly available in real-time? |  |
|  | Does your platform support raw data export to an external location? |  |
|  | Are raw data outputs built to support machine learning and data science use cases? |  |
|  | Does your platform support data forwarding through webhooks? |  |
|  | Does your platform support data replay for historical analytics? |  |
|  | How many third-party destinations in the catalog are supported via server-side connections? |  |
|  | How many of your third-party integrations support a bi-directional flow? Which ones? |  |
|  | Can the system support the removal of client-side ad pixels? |  |

## SOLUTION OVERVIEW


### The DÂ·engage Customer Driven Marketing Platform (CDMP)




The DÂ·engage platform is a full end-to-end solution that requires no external technologies to function. The technology contains everything Sisal will need to deliver exceptional customer experiences through all key channels. Following is a list of the core components which make up the DÂ·engage Customer Driven Marketing Platform:

### Data Unification â€“ Customer Data Platform

With a Customer Data Platform at its cores the DÂ·engage allows you to collect, unify and activate complete profiles of every known and anonymous customers. DÂ·engage enables integration with virtually any and all data sources through robust web services, pre-built integrations, and a simple user interface for file uploads. This will allow your team to use actionable data stored in customer profiles, account history, click-through behavior, and other information when segmenting known and anonymous contacts, using dynamic content, and personalizing campaigns.


#### Data Management

One of the hallmarks of the DÂ·engage platform is the Data Model that will enable Sisal to utilize any data, from any data source, in its standard format to drive more targeted, relevant, personalized campaigns. Built on an open relational data model, the DÂ·engage Customer Data Platform will enable you to use data without spending the effort (and cost) to make it fit into a pre-set structure that so many other platforms force you into. And this flexibility, enabled by the data model, will allow you to speak to customers as individuals, respond to events and behaviors as they happen, and avoid the reliance on IT projects to execute your campaigns and manage your sales pipeline.

Flexible relational databases

This team is comprised of technical, strategy, and product experts who have years of experience in implementing DÂ·engage Products. DÂ·engageâ€™s onboarding approach is comprised of the following phases (some of which are performed in-parallel):




**Phase 1 â€“ Initiation and Planning**


The DÂ·engage Project Management team will partner with you to ensure that the implementation of your Customer Driven Marketing Platform is perfectly configured for your specific marketing and business goals. The initial phase of the onboarding project is to spend time working with the client to properly analyze all the necessary technical and business requirements for the specific implementation.

**Initial Kickoff Meeting and Requirements Gathering**

The Project Management will start with a kickoff meeting with the client to evaluate the needs and requirements for the business.
Typical aspects that are discussed in this phase include:
Current data model
Current programs and campaigns
Segment discovery
Problem areas
Long-term programs and campaigns
Overall strategy and Key Performance Indicators
Unsubscribe management
Integrations with existing platforms/systems and 3rd party solution


**Phase 2 â€“ Setup and Installation**


The setup and installation phase consist of setting up your accounts, access configuration with MFA and IP restrictions, data flow setup, SDK setup and testing.


**The main tasks in this stage include:**


Account Setup
Review and migrate existing campaigns
Plan and create new campaigns and personalized experiences
Data flow configuration and implementation
Web and Mobile SDK setup and testing

**Creating new Campaigns and Building a Personalization Roadmap:**

- Define overall contact strategy and build a personalization roadmap
- Define data/targeting strategy
- Define Sales Pipeline Management steps and KPIâ€™s
- Prioritize quick win campaigns and automated personalized engagement opportunities
- Design, build and test batch and triggered (Business as Usual) campaigns
- Personalized experience and AI based segmentation workshops

**Phase 3 â€“ Training and Testing**

Upon completion of the implementation phase it is expected that your account to be fully configured. This will be verified by executing the previously developed test plans from the above phases to validate application functionality.

DÂ·engage Project Management team will provide development and end-user manuals to Sisal.Â Technical and end-user training will also be organized at this stage. Additionally, a Pilot will be deployed at the end of this stage prior to deployment. The Project results and executive summary will be prepared and delivered to the Sisal team.


**Key deliverables in this phase include:**



**Testing**

System testing
User acceptance testing


**Running the Pilot**

Run Pilot campaigns across channels
Deliver and present project results & executive summary

**Documentation and Training**

System development documents
Development & end-user manuals
Technical and end-user training



**Phase 4 â€“ Deployment and Go Live**


Once all testing has been successfully completed, the steady state migration of the live application data will begin. The exact methodology used to carry out this task will be determined during the initial planning phase and will include appropriate roll back procedures should an unexpected event occur.
After Go-Live, the following work will be required on an ongoing basis. Again, DÂ·engage can help with these activities through a collaborative model or Sisal can drive these activities directly through the interface.
- Build and setup of automated campaigns as defined in the contact strategy and personalization roadmap
- Monitoring, testing and optimization of campaigns and personalized experiences
- Adding/optimization of data synchronization
- Check all reports for revenue generation, deliverability, etc.

### Maintenance and Support (Ongoing support)


The standard plan provides customers the following services and ongoing technical support:

Access to DÂ·engageâ€™s online customer service portal to create support tickets.
Email assistance for product related technical issues during DÂ·engageâ€™s working hours on a five-days a week basis.
Out of hours support
Account management

**Response and Resolution Service Levels**

Response means positive acknowledgement from DÂ·engage that DÂ·engage has received information from the end user regarding a question with the Software and that DÂ·engage is investigating the details. Resolution means closure of a customer issue.



### DÂ·engageâ€™s Risk Management Approach




Each step is detailed below;
Risk Management Planningâ€”The initial work performed to identify the risk management approach to be used on the project.
Risk Identificationâ€” The potential sources of risks both initially and on an ongoing basis identified.
Qualitative Risk Assessmentâ€”The process of actually assessing the risks against the project assessment criteria.
Quantitative Risk Assessmentâ€”The process of budgeting the risks against the project assessment criteria.
Risk Response Planningâ€”The creative process of identifying the risk response strategies that will be used and the detailed risk response plan(s) (what will be done or not done) for each risk identified. This planning includes identifying the trigger event that will cause the risk response plan to be executed.
Risk Monitoring and Controlâ€”The process of monitoring for a risk event occurrence, reassessing the risk (likelihood and consequence) and monitoring the performance of the risk response plan and reporting the results.

This team is comprised of technical, deliverability, strategy, and product experts who have years of experience in new DÂ·engage implementations. ## Company Profile



### About DÂ·engage


DÂ·engage is the brainchild of Aman Dotani and Osman TufanoÄŸullarÄ±, both of whom are pioneers and shapers of the MarTech industry since the early 2000â€™s. Between them they have organically grown, transformed, co-founded and headed independent leaders of our industry: Emailvision (SmartFocus); Emarsys (SAP); euromessage/Related Digital (Dogus Group); transforming them from small parochial providers into global multi-million-dollar overachievers subsequently bought by much larger corporations. Spotting a gap in the MarTech environment and wishing to maintain the flexibility and agility of being an independent provider, the DÂ·engage company was established in early 2018.

The team devoted close to 15 months in pure development, entirely self-funded, before seriously launching the platform in 2019. Thanks to the pedigree of the technology, our first clients were heavy hitters such as Akbank, N11.com, Sportsmaster.ru, Bilyoner.com, Nesine.com whose success with us allowed further expansion into the markets of Turkey, Russia, LATAM, APAC, Middle East and Africa. 2020 and subsequently 2021 has seen the business ramp up significantly in turnover, team size and client base. 

We currently have almost 100 team members worldwide lead by hugely experienced industry experts allowing us to further expand the business into Europe, and North America. Serving more than 130 clients from our registered offices in London, Moscow, Istanbul, Dubai, and Islamabad we continue to grow exponentially and completely organically.




### Customer References


DÂ·engage is used by customer-centric brands across a wide range of industries. Using DÂ·engage, these organizations gain a deep understanding of their prospects and customersâ€™ expectations and automate the delivery of highly personalized engagement across all channels and devices.

We are dedicated to helping our clients succeed. Our experienced team of professionals in digital marketing, IT, data science and client services become a highly skilled extension of our clientsâ€™ organization helping them achieve their unique goals and deliver more valuable customer engagement.





## SUMMARY


Our most successful customers leverage the full breadth of our customer data management, marketing automation and cross-channel engagement platform by focusing on data, personalization, reporting and optimization. They integrate all their corporate data systems with the DÂ·engage Platform, develop competencies around customer/prospect targeting and segmentation, sales pipeline management and look to implement as many fully automated and triggered customer journeys as possible.
Our most successful customers also treat the DÂ·engage services teams as extensions of their organizations, leveraging the different services teams to help.
Clients who move to DÂ·engage who send predominantly broadcast emails 2-3 times a week see significant increases in revenue by following the New School Marketing Approach in reducing the number of broadcast emails, introducing targeted/personalized campaigns and automating, testing and optimizing multi-stage journeys on an ongoing basis.

This ensures that the segment's audience remains up-to-date based on the defined refresh schedule.<br><br>Real-Time Segments (System Default Behavior): Real-time segments are calculated instantly at the moment they are called upon. | How many turn-key intergrations do you have? |  | Full Compliance | Turnkey Integrations (60)<br>- Ecommerce Platforms: Magento, Shopify, PrestaShop, Neto<br>- Data Warehouses: Google BiqQuery, Amazon Redshift, Azure Data Lake, Oracle BlueKai, Dreamio<br>- CRM: Dynamics 365, Salesforce<br>- Advertising/Retargeting: Google Customer Match, Facebook Custom Audience, Criteo<br>- Surveys: Typeform, Survey Monkey<br>- Recommendation/Growth: Segmentify, WIS<br>- Bonus Engine: OpenTag<br>- SMS/WhatsApp: 38 diffferent operators globally including Codec, Infobip and CM<br>- Business Intelligence: Tableau<br>- Analytics: Adjust, MixPanel, Adobe |
| Are there multiple ways to integrate (tags, APIs, SDKs?) |  | Full Compliance | The CDP offers multiple methods for integrating data, allowing seamless connectivity to the platform. These integration options include APIs, SDKs, and Tags (List Tags/Key-Value Pair Tags). <br><br>With APIs, developers can leverage a programming interface to securely transfer data to and from the CDP. SDKs provide software development kits that simplify the integration process and offer additional functionality. <br>Tags, such as List Tags or Key-Value Pair Tags, enable flexible and customizable data organization, making it easier to categorize and manage data within the CDP. <br><br>These various integration approaches provide users with diverse and efficient means to connect and integrate their data with the CDP. |
| Is there an added cost for additional integrations? |  | Full Compliance | We do not charge for integrations that will add value to our marketplace and will be used by other clients. If the inetgration consists of a custom integration unique to the client we do charge man/day cost to cover our expenses. |
| Is there a process for developing additional integrations? |  | Full Compliance | Integrations are currently prioritized based on clients' and prospects demands from various regions. <br><br>1. Requirements Gathering: The product team collaborates with clients and prospects to gather detailed requirements for the desired integrations. This step involves understanding their specific needs, objectives, and any constraints or dependencies.<br><br>2. Design and Planning: Software architects and designers work closely to create a high-level design and architectural plan for incorporating the new integrations. This includes evaluating the impact on the existing system, identifying potential risks, and defining the integration interfaces.<br><br>3. Development: Development teams start implementing the integration features based on the design and architectural plan. They follow coding best practices, adhere to coding standards, and perform regular code reviews to ensure the quality and maintainability of the integration code.<br><br>4. Testing: The integrated features undergo rigorous testing to identify and fix any bugs or issues. <br><br>5. Deployment: Once the integrations have passed testing and quality assurance, they are deployed to the production environment. This step involves coordinating with operations teams to ensure a smooth and successful deployment, and monitoring the system closely after deployment to verify its stability and performance.<br><br>6. Documentation and Training: Documentation is created to guide users, clients, and support teams on how to utilize the new integrations effectively. <br><br>7. Maintenance and Support: The integration features are regularly maintained and monitored for any issues or updates. Support teams are available to address any questions or problems that arise and to provide ongoing assistance to clients and users. |
| Can users customize existing marketplace integrations? |  | Full Compliance | Existing data structure for marketplace integrations can be customized and extended based on business requirements. e.g. Additional data sources such as offline data can be integrated with shopify plugin to unify online/offline customer, order and demographic data |
| Does the CDP make data available for analysis with BI tools. (ie, formats, timeline, etc) |  | Partial Compliance | CDP provides data in multiple formats, such as CSV, JSON, or SQL, making it compatible with various BI tools. The availability of data for analysis depends on the integration and syncing processes between the CDP and the BI tools. Typically, data is made available in near real-time or on a scheduled basis, depending on the configuration and requirements. |
| Data |  |  |  |
| Can the data from the CDP be exported? |  | Full Compliance | In the CDP, there are multiple ways to export data, providing flexibility and options for extracting data as needed:<br><br>REST APIs: The CDP offers REST APIs that allow users to extract data, specifically channel responses and segments. These APIs enable programmatic access to retrieve data in a structured format for further analysis or integration with external systems.<br><br>ETL Automation Center: The ETL Automation Center within the CDP provides extensive data extraction capabilities. Users can utilize this feature to extract data in various ways. It supports exporting data through file transfers to FTP sites, as well as writing data to external databases that support ODBC, JDBC (e.g., Oracle, SQL Server, Postgres), or known cloud databases such as AWS Redshift, Google BigQuery, or Azure Data Warehouse.<br><br>Export Node in Journeys: Within the journeys module, the Export Node enables the export of data from the platform. This allows users to extract and export relevant data points or entire datasets at specific points in the customer journey for further analysis or integration with external systems.<br><br>Export to Advertising Networks: The CDP also supports data exports to advertising networks such as Facebook and Google. This enables seamless integration and data synchronization between the CDP and advertising platforms, ensuring consistent and targeted advertising campaigns.<br><br>By offering these various data export methods, the CDP ensures that users can efficiently extract and utilize their data in diverse ways, enabling analysis, integration, and effective targeting across multiple channels and platforms. |
| Do you provide access to raw event-level data? |  | Full Compliance | The CDP provides a SQL Editor that allows end users to interact with any table, including raw-level event data. |

| is each CDP data isolated from other customers' environments ? Customer A Profiles and Data is Totally separated and processed in isolation from Customer B Profiles and Data ? |  | Full Compliance | The various schemas are completely isolated from one another, with each schema being accessed by specific database users. These users are automatically managed by the platform. |
| Is there the ability to delete a user from all records? |  | Full Compliance | Contact Delete functions is used to delete the contact from all related records |

| Does your CDP provide opt in/out control for the end visitor? |  | Full Compliance | The CDP provides various forms of opt-in and opt-out for end visitors, contacts and users:<br>- Channel level opt-out: unsubscribe management from different communication channels<br>- Customized Unsubsribe Pages: Multi-Channel opt-out with additional information such as opt-ot reasons and more<br>- Brand Level Unsubscribe Pages: Unsubscribe option from different brands and channels used by multi-brand companies such as FMCG's<br>- Subscription Forms: Collect opt-in with list subscriptions from end visitors through forms<br><br>Within the context of the CDP (Customer Data Platform), there are multiple fields available, such as contact_status, email_permission, sms_permission, push_permission, email_status, and more. These fields serve the purpose of managing and regulating the consent given by end visitors. |
| How does your CDP integrate with existing CMP (Consent Management Platforms) ? |  | Full Compliance | The CDP integrates with existing Consent Management Systems through RestAPI's |

While we don't have any compliance barrier to apply for the security certifications. | Are you GDPR and CCPA compliant? |  | Partial Compliance | GDPR Compliant, same reason as above (line 57). We do not have any clients that specifically required CCPA compliance |
| Do you support MFA/2FA (multi-factor authentication) and SSO? |  | Full Compliance | the CDP supports MFA/2FA via Email. It also supports LDAP for SSO |

| Is data encrypted in transit? |  | Full Compliance | yes, CDP supports enryption via FTP or REST API on transit |
| Is data encrypted at rest? |  | Partial Compliance | only PII data is encrypted at Rest |
| What controls do you have to restrict access to PII for Users and for Vendors? |  | Full Compliance | To restrict access to personally identifiable information (PII) for both users and vendors within a system, CDP implements various controls.<br><br>1. User authentication and authorization: Implement strong authentication mechanisms to verify the identity of users accessing the system. This includes username and password combinations, multi-factor authentication (MFA). Authorization control is in place to grant appropriate access privileges based on user roles and responsibilities.<br><br>2. Role-based access control (RBAC): Assign specific roles to users and vendors based on their responsibilities and permissions. RBAC ensures that access to PII is limited to authorized individuals who need it for their designated tasks. Fine-grained access controls is implemented to allow access only to the necessary PII elements for each role.<br><br>3. Data masking: Employ data masking techniques to protect PII both at rest (stored data) and in transit (data being transmitted over networks). <br><br>4. Data access logs and monitoring: CDP maintains detailed logs of PII access activities, including user interactions. Monitor and analyze these logs to detect any suspicious or unauthorized access attempts. Regularly review access logs to ensure compliance and identify any potential security breaches. |
| What controls do you have to restrict access to API's? |  | Full Compliance | To restrict access to APIs (Application Programming Interfaces), the CDP implements :<br><br>Authentication and authorization: strong authentication mechanisms to verify the identity of users or applications accessing the API. This involves username and password combinations. Authorization controls determine the level of access granted to authenticated entities based on their roles and permissions.<br><br>Rate limiting: Set limits on the number of API requests that can be made within a specific time period. Rate limiting helps prevent abuse, unauthorized access, and excessive usage that could impact the API's performance. It also helps manage and allocate resources effectively.<br><br>IP whitelisting: Control access to APIs based on IP addresses. IP whitelisting allows only specific IP addresses or IP ranges to access the API<br><br>Secure protocols and encryption: APIs should be accessed over secure protocols such as HTTPS to ensure data privacy and integrity. Transport Layer Security (TLS) encryption can be employed to protect the communication between clients and the API server.<br><br> |
| What controls do you have to restrict access writing Code? |  |  | The CDP provides the capability to execute code, known as Code Snippets, to create highly personalized messages within the platform. To prevent abuse or misuse of these code snippets, the CDP employs a maker/checker mechanism. This mechanism ensures that there is a two-step validation process in place before the code snippets are executed, thereby maintaining control and preventing any potential issues that may arise from improper use of the code. |
| Technology |  |  |  |
| Programming languages used for Frontend and Backend along with major architecture concepts |  | Full Compliance | <br>The backend microservices have been developed using .NET Core (C#), which provides a versatile framework for creating robust applications. This choice of technology allows the platform to operate seamlessly on both Linux and Windows environments. However, our team primarily utilizes the Linux environment in our cloud deployments.<br><br>On the frontend side, Vue.js has been utilized for development. Vue.js is a popular JavaScript framework that enables the creation of interactive and responsive user interfaces.<br><br>The platform follows a Microservice Architecture, which offers numerous benefits in terms of scalability, flexibility, and deployment options. The microservices are containerized using Docker, allowing for easy management and deployment. Additionally, Kubernetes is another viable option for running all the microservices, providing orchestration and scaling capabilities for containerized applications.<br><br>With the combination of .NET Core, Vue.js, and Microservice Architecture, the platform is designed to be highly adaptable and deployable in various cloud or on-premise environments, providing flexibility and scalability to meet the specific needs of different deployment scenarios. |
| Databases |  | Full Compliance | We have developed an exceptional mixed of relational + event database, incorporating the expertise of our top-notch consultants. Our database is built on a powerful combination of PostgreSQL and Distributed PostgreSQL at its core, enabling us to seamlessly operate both relational and event databases at a large scale, functioning as a unified database.<br><br>This innovative fusion of technologies empowers us to effectively manage diverse data requirements while ensuring scalability and optimal performance. By leveraging the synergies between PostgreSQL and Distributed PostgreSQL, our mixed database provides a cohesive solution that combines the strengths of relational and event-driven databases.<br><br>Our state-of-the-art mixed relational + event database represents the culmination of extensive research and collaboration with industry-leading consultants. It offers a unified platform capable of efficiently handling large-scale data operations, delivering impressive performance and scalability. |
| Top 3 supported Cloud providers and supported deploymment models |  | Full Compliance | Amazon Web Services (AWS), Google Cloud, Microsoft Azure - We can support all major cloud providers.  |
| Is their any cloud specific technologies used or platform highly depend on ? |  | Full Compliance | The platform is designed to be completely agnostic of any cloud-specific technologies, ensuring independence from any particular platform. This independence empowers us to deploy the platform in on-premise environments or private cloud environments seamlessly.<br><br>By avoiding reliance on cloud-specific technologies, we have the freedom to choose the deployment environment that best suits clients' needs. Whether it's an on-premise infrastructure or a private cloud solution, the platform can be deployed without any limitations.<br><br>The platform's architecture is built to be versatile, enabling deployment and operation in various environments. This flexibility allows the client to select the most suitable deployment option, ensuring compatibility and adaptability regardless of the platform the client chooses. |
